From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n9\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.0, F50.1, F50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess what finally pushed me to make the appointment is my eating has just been… kind of inconsistent for a few months. It’s not dramatic, I don’t think, but I’ll go through stretches where I skip dinner and lose weight without really trying. And then other nights I’ll just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking just for myself feels sort of pointless. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing I should be coming in for, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight during these periods?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean… I wouldn’t say I’ve been dieting or trying to lose weight. It’s more that my appetite just sort of drops off, and I’ll skip dinner without really meaning to. Then I notice my weight’s down a bit and I feel embarrassed, like I should be managing meals better. Other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for just me feels… I don’t know, kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you don't eat a full meal or when you're not eating what you think you should be eating?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I know missing a meal isn’t some terrible thing, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. But it kind of ties into this other loop in my head… like I keep replaying the marriage and thinking I must have failed at something really basic. Part of me knows relationships are more complicated than that, but I still end up feeling like I didn’t manage even simple things right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly, but I have lost weight without trying. It’s more like my appetite comes and goes, and during the stretches where I skip dinner, I’ll notice my weight has dropped a bit. Then other nights I’m just sort of picking at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. I’m not sure if that counts as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your appetite or eating habits change when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or sad?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does change when I’m stressed or sad. I don’t always notice it right away, but my appetite gets kind of unpredictable. There are stretches where I just skip dinner without really planning to, and then I’ll realize I’ve lost some weight without trying.\n\nOther nights I’ll sort of pick at crackers or cereal, not because I think that’s a proper meal, but because cooking just for myself feels… I don’t know, pointless. I feel a little embarrassed saying it that way, but I guess that’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think I’m afraid of gaining weight exactly. It’s more the opposite, if anything… what finally made me come in is that my appetite has been all over the place for months. I’ll go through stretches where I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. I don’t know if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “lost control” is exactly how I’d put it… it’s not like I’m overeating and can’t stop. It’s more that my appetite feels all over the place. I’ll go through stretches where I just skip dinner and then realize I’ve lost weight without meaning to, and other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. I know I’ve kind of said this already, but it’s more that my eating has been inconsistent for months. Sometimes I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating because you're worried about the food itself, like its nutritional content or potential to make you sick?\n\nPatient: No, not really because I’m worried the food is bad for me or will make me sick. It’s more… I don’t always feel like bothering with a real meal. Sometimes I’ll skip dinner, or just have crackers or cereal, and then later I notice I’ve lost some weight without meaning to. It’s been kind of all over the place for months. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I don’t always notice it right away, but my appetite gets kind of unpredictable. There are stretches where I just skip dinner without really planning to, and then I’ll realize I’ve lost some weight without trying.\n\nOther nights I’ll sort of pick at crackers or cereal, not because I think that’s a proper meal, but because cooking just for myself feels… I don’t know, pointless. I feel a little embarrassed saying it that way, but I guess that’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think I’m afraid of gaining weight exactly. It’s more the opposite, if anything… what finally made me come in is that my appetite has been all over the place for months. I’ll go through stretches where I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. I don’t know if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “lost control” is exactly how I’d put it… it’s not like I’m overeating and can’t stop. It’s more that my appetite feels all over the place. I’ll go through stretches where I just skip dinner and then realize I’ve lost weight without meaning to, and other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. I know I’ve kind of said this already, but it’s more that my eating has been inconsistent for months. Sometimes I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating because you're worried about the food itself, like its nutritional content or potential to make you sick?\n\nPatient: No, not really because I’m worried the food is bad for me or will make me sick. It’s more… I don’t always feel like bothering with a real meal. Sometimes I’ll skip dinner, or just have crackers or cereal, and then later I notice I’ve lost some weight without meaning to. It’s been kind of all over the place for months. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape plays a role in how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I don’t love every little thing about getting older, like anyone, but my weight or shape isn’t really what determines how I feel about myself. Part of me knows relationships are more complicated than that, but I still end up feeling like I didn’t manage even simple things right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly, but I have lost weight without trying. It’s more like my appetite comes and goes, and during the stretches where I skip dinner, I’ll notice my weight has dropped a bit. Then other nights I’m just sort of picking at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. I’m not sure if that counts as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your appetite or eating habits change when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or sad?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does change when I’m stressed or sad. I don’t always notice it right away, but my appetite gets kind of unpredictable. There are stretches where I just skip dinner without really planning to, and then I’ll realize I’ve lost some weight without trying.\n\nOther nights I’ll sort of pick at crackers or cereal, not because I think that’s a proper meal, but because cooking just for myself feels… I don’t know, pointless. I feel a little embarrassed saying it that way, but I guess that’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think I’m afraid of gaining weight exactly. It’s more the opposite, if anything… what finally made me come in is that my appetite has been all over the place for months. I’ll go through stretches where I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. I don’t know if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “lost control” is exactly how I’d put it… it’s not like I’m overeating and can’t stop. It’s more that my appetite feels all over the place. I’ll go through stretches where I just skip dinner and then realize I’ve lost weight without meaning to, and other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. I know I’ve kind of said this already, but it’s more that my eating has been inconsistent for months. Sometimes I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating because you're worried about the food itself, like its nutritional content or potential to make you sick?\n\nPatient: No, not really because I’m worried the food is bad for me or will make me sick. It’s more… I don’t always feel like bothering with a real meal. Sometimes I’ll skip dinner, or just have crackers or cereal, and then later I notice I’ve lost some weight without meaning to. It’s been kind of all over the place for months. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape plays a role in how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I don’t love every little thing about getting older, like anyone, but my weight or shape isn’t really what determines how I feel about myself. At least, I haven’t noticed that being a big factor. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel uncomfortable eating in front of other people?\n\nPatient: Not especially, no. I mean, I might feel a little self-conscious if everyone is watching me or something, but I don’t usually avoid eating with people because of it. I haven’t really noticed that being a problem. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that eating a normal meal will cause you to gain weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s really it. I’m not usually thinking, “If I eat this meal, I’ll gain weight.” It’s more that I just… don’t always feel up to eating much. But the weight gain worry isn’t something I’ve noticed, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

It’s not dramatic, I don’t think, but I’ll go through stretches where I skip dinner and lose weight without really trying. And then other nights I’ll just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking just for myself feels sort of pointless. I’m not sure if that’s the kind of thing I should be coming in for, but it’s been bothering me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been trying to lose weight during these periods?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean… I wouldn’t say I’ve been dieting or trying to lose weight. It’s more that my appetite just sort of drops off, and I’ll skip dinner without really meaning to. Then I notice my weight’s down a bit and I feel embarrassed, like I should be managing meals better. Other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for just me feels… I don’t know, kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you don't eat a full meal or when you're not eating what you think you should be eating?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I know missing a meal isn’t some terrible thing, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. But it kind of ties into this other loop in my head… like I keep replaying the marriage and thinking I must have failed at something really basic. Part of me knows relationships are more complicated than that, but I still end up feeling like I didn’t manage even simple things right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly, but I have lost weight without trying. It’s more like my appetite comes and goes, and during the stretches where I skip dinner, I’ll notice my weight has dropped a bit. Then other nights I’m just sort of picking at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. I’m not sure if that counts as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your appetite or eating habits change when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or sad?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does change when I’m stressed or sad. I don’t always notice it right away, but my appetite gets kind of unpredictable. There are stretches where I just skip dinner without really planning to, and then I’ll realize I’ve lost some weight without trying.\n\nOther nights I’ll sort of pick at crackers or cereal, not because I think that’s a proper meal, but because cooking just for myself feels… I don’t know, pointless. I feel a little embarrassed saying it that way, but I guess that’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think I’m afraid of gaining weight exactly. It’s more the opposite, if anything… what finally made me come in is that my appetite has been all over the place for months. I’ll go through stretches where I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. I don’t know if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “lost control” is exactly how I’d put it… it’s not like I’m overeating and can’t stop. It’s more that my appetite feels all over the place. I’ll go through stretches where I just skip dinner and then realize I’ve lost weight without meaning to, and other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating because you're worried about the food itself, like its nutritional content or potential to make you sick?\n\nPatient: No, not really because I’m worried the food is bad for me or will make me sick. It’s been kind of all over the place for months. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape plays a role in how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. At least, I haven’t noticed that being a big factor. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel uncomfortable eating in front of other people?\n\nPatient: Not especially, no. I mean, I might feel a little self-conscious if everyone is watching me or something, but I don’t usually avoid eating with people because of it. I haven’t really noticed that being a problem. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that eating a normal meal will cause you to gain weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s really it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry that if you start eating a normal amount, you won't be able to stop eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s something I worry about. I mean, once I’m eating I can usually stop normally… I’m not afraid it’ll get out of control or anything. At least, I haven’t noticed that. Is that helpful?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean… I wouldn’t say I’ve been dieting or trying to lose weight. It’s more that my appetite just sort of drops off, and I’ll skip dinner without really meaning to. Then I notice my weight’s down a bit and I feel embarrassed, like I should be managing meals better. Other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for just me feels… I don’t know, kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you don't eat a full meal or when you're not eating what you think you should be eating?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I know missing a meal isn’t some terrible thing, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. But it kind of ties into this other loop in my head… like I keep replaying the marriage and thinking I must have failed at something really basic. Part of me knows relationships are more complicated than that, but I still end up feeling like I didn’t manage even simple things right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly, but I have lost weight without trying. It’s more like my appetite comes and goes, and during the stretches where I skip dinner, I’ll notice my weight has dropped a bit. Then other nights I’m just sort of picking at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. I’m not sure if that counts as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your appetite or eating habits change when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or sad?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does change when I’m stressed or sad. I don’t always notice it right away, but my appetite gets kind of unpredictable. There are stretches where I just skip dinner without really planning to, and then I’ll realize I’ve lost some weight without trying.\n\nOther nights I’ll sort of pick at crackers or cereal, not because I think that’s a proper meal, but because cooking just for myself feels… I don’t know, pointless. I feel a little embarrassed saying it that way, but I guess that’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think I’m afraid of gaining weight exactly. It’s more the opposite, if anything… what finally made me come in is that my appetite has been all over the place for months. I’ll go through stretches where I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. I don’t know if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “lost control” is exactly how I’d put it… it’s not like I’m overeating and can’t stop. It’s more that my appetite feels all over the place. I’ll go through stretches where I just skip dinner and then realize I’ve lost weight without meaning to, and other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. I know I’ve kind of said this already, but it’s more that my eating has been inconsistent for months. Sometimes I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating because you're worried about the food itself, like its nutritional content or potential to make you sick?\n\nPatient: No, not really because I’m worried the food is bad for me or will make me sick. It’s more… I don’t always feel like bothering with a real meal. Sometimes I’ll skip dinner, or just have crackers or cereal, and then later I notice I’ve lost some weight without meaning to. It’s been kind of all over the place for months. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape plays a role in how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I don’t love every little thing about getting older, like anyone, but my weight or shape isn’t really what determines how I feel about myself. At least, I haven’t noticed that being a big factor. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel uncomfortable eating in front of other people?\n\nPatient: Not especially, no. I mean, I might feel a little self-conscious if everyone is watching me or something, but I don’t usually avoid eating with people because of it. I haven’t really noticed that being a problem. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that eating a normal meal will cause you to gain weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s really it. I’m not usually thinking, “If I eat this meal, I’ll gain weight.” It’s more that I just… don’t always feel up to eating much. But the weight gain worry isn’t something I’ve noticed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating is ever influenced by a fear of not being able to stop once you start?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I don’t really have that feeling of, “If I start eating I won’t be able to stop.” It’s more… I just eat what I can, I guess. I haven’t noticed that fear being a part of it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry that if you start eating a normal amount, you won't be able to stop eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s something I worry about. I mean, once I’m eating I can usually stop normally… I’m not afraid it’ll get out of control or anything. At least, I haven’t noticed that. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious about eating because you might lose control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I feel anxious about that. I don’t really worry that I’ll lose control once I start eating. It’s more straightforward than that, I think… but I haven’t noticed that kind of fear. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating a normal meal could lead to eating too much?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really think of it that way. A normal meal doesn’t make me feel like it would turn into eating too much. I mean… I suppose I don’t always think about it that carefully, but I haven’t noticed that fear. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be upset if you gained back any weight you've lost?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d be especially upset about gaining weight back, no. It’s more that my appetite has just been… kind of inconsistent for months. Sometimes I skip dinner without really meaning to and lose a little weight, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for just me feels sort of pointless. I don’t know if that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are related to trying to look a certain way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really about trying to look a certain way. I mean, I’m not dieting or aiming for a size or anything. It’s more… my appetite has just been unpredictable for months. Some evenings I end up skipping dinner and lose some weight without meaning to, and other nights I just have crackers or cereal because making a meal just for myself feels kind of pointless. Then I notice my weight’s down a bit and I feel embarrassed, like I should be managing meals better. Other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for just me feels… I don’t know, kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you don't eat a full meal or when you're not eating what you think you should be eating?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I know missing a meal isn’t some terrible thing, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. But it kind of ties into this other loop in my head… like I keep replaying the marriage and thinking I must have failed at something really basic. Part of me knows relationships are more complicated than that, but I still end up feeling like I didn’t manage even simple things right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly, but I have lost weight without trying. It’s more like my appetite comes and goes, and during the stretches where I skip dinner, I’ll notice my weight has dropped a bit. Then other nights I’m just sort of picking at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. I’m not sure if that counts as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your appetite or eating habits change when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or sad?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does change when I’m stressed or sad. I don’t always notice it right away, but my appetite gets kind of unpredictable. There are stretches where I just skip dinner without really planning to, and then I’ll realize I’ve lost some weight without trying.\n\nOther nights I’ll sort of pick at crackers or cereal, not because I think that’s a proper meal, but because cooking just for myself feels… I don’t know, pointless. I feel a little embarrassed saying it that way, but I guess that’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I don’t think I’m afraid of gaining weight exactly. It’s more the opposite, if anything… what finally made me come in is that my appetite has been all over the place for months. I’ll go through stretches where I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. I don’t know if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “lost control” is exactly how I’d put it… it’s not like I’m overeating and can’t stop. It’s more that my appetite feels all over the place. I’ll go through stretches where I just skip dinner and then realize I’ve lost weight without meaning to, and other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. I know I’ve kind of said this already, but it’s more that my eating has been inconsistent for months. Sometimes I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating because you're worried about the food itself, like its nutritional content or potential to make you sick?\n\nPatient: No, not really because I’m worried the food is bad for me or will make me sick. It’s more… I don’t always feel like bothering with a real meal. Sometimes I’ll skip dinner, or just have crackers or cereal, and then later I notice I’ve lost some weight without meaning to. It’s been kind of all over the place for months. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape plays a role in how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I don’t love every little thing about getting older, like anyone, but my weight or shape isn’t really what determines how I feel about myself. At least, I haven’t noticed that being a big factor. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel uncomfortable eating in front of other people?\n\nPatient: Not especially, no. I mean, I might feel a little self-conscious if everyone is watching me or something, but I don’t usually avoid eating with people because of it. I haven’t really noticed that being a problem. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that eating a normal meal will cause you to gain weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s really it. I’m not usually thinking, “If I eat this meal, I’ll gain weight.” It’s more that I just… don’t always feel up to eating much. But the weight gain worry isn’t something I’ve noticed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating is ever influenced by a fear of not being able to stop once you start?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I don’t really have that feeling of, “If I start eating I won’t be able to stop.” It’s more… I just eat what I can, I guess. I haven’t noticed that fear being a part of it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry that if you start eating a normal amount, you won't be able to stop eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s something I worry about. I mean, once I’m eating I can usually stop normally… I’m not afraid it’ll get out of control or anything. At least, I haven’t noticed that. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious about eating because you might lose control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I feel anxious about that. I don’t really worry that I’ll lose control once I start eating. It’s more straightforward than that, I think… but I haven’t noticed that kind of fear. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating a normal meal could lead to eating too much?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really think of it that way. A normal meal doesn’t make me feel like it would turn into eating too much. I mean… I suppose I don’t always think about it that carefully, but I haven’t noticed that fear. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be upset if you gained back any weight you've lost?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d be especially upset about gaining weight back, no. It’s more that my appetite has just been… kind of inconsistent for months. Sometimes I skip dinner without really meaning to and lose a little weight, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for just me feels sort of pointless. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess what finally pushed me to make the appointment is my eating has just been… kind of inconsistent for a few months. And then other nights I’ll just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking just for myself feels sort of pointless. I mean… I wouldn’t say I’ve been dieting or trying to lose weight. It’s more that my appetite just sort of drops off, and I’ll skip dinner without really meaning to. Then I notice my weight’s down a bit and I feel embarrassed, like I should be managing meals better. Other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for just me feels… I don’t know, kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you don't eat a full meal or when you're not eating what you think you should be eating?\n\nPatient: A little, yes. I mean, I know missing a meal isn’t some terrible thing, and I don’t want to make it sound bigger than it is. But it kind of ties into this other loop in my head… like I keep replaying the marriage and thinking I must have failed at something really basic. Part of me knows relationships are more complicated than that, but I still end up feeling like I didn’t manage even simple things right. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Some, yes… I don’t know if I’d call it significant exactly, but I have lost weight without trying. It’s more like my appetite comes and goes, and during the stretches where I skip dinner, I’ll notice my weight has dropped a bit. Then other nights I’m just sort of picking at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. I’m not sure if that counts as significant.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your appetite or eating habits change when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or sad?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think it does change when I’m stressed or sad. I don’t always notice it right away, but my appetite gets kind of unpredictable. There are stretches where I just skip dinner without really planning to, and then I’ll realize I’ve lost some weight without trying.\n\nOther nights I’ll sort of pick at crackers or cereal, not because I think that’s a proper meal, but because cooking just for myself feels… I don’t know, pointless. I feel a little embarrassed saying it that way, but I guess that’s part of what finally made me come in.\n\nDoctor: Are you ever worried about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I’ll go through stretches where I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. I don’t know if that answers what you mean.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you've lost control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: I don’t think “lost control” is exactly how I’d put it… it’s not like I’m overeating and can’t stop. It’s more that my appetite feels all over the place. I’ll go through stretches where I just skip dinner and then realize I’ve lost weight without meaning to, and other nights I’ll just have crackers or cereal because cooking for myself feels kind of pointless. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight?\n\nPatient: No, I’m not afraid of gaining weight. I know I’ve kind of said this already, but it’s more that my eating has been inconsistent for months. Sometimes I skip dinner and then notice I’ve lost weight without trying, and other nights I just pick at crackers or cereal because making a real meal for myself feels… unnecessary, I guess. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever avoid eating because you're worried about the food itself, like its nutritional content or potential to make you sick?\n\nPatient: No, not really because I’m worried the food is bad for me or will make me sick. It’s more… I don’t always feel like bothering with a real meal. Sometimes I’ll skip dinner, or just have crackers or cereal, and then later I notice I’ve lost some weight without meaning to. It’s been kind of all over the place for months. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your weight or body shape plays a role in how you feel about yourself?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I don’t love every little thing about getting older, like anyone, but my weight or shape isn’t really what determines how I feel about myself. At least, I haven’t noticed that being a big factor. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel uncomfortable eating in front of other people?\n\nPatient: Not especially, no. I mean, I might feel a little self-conscious if everyone is watching me or something, but I don’t usually avoid eating with people because of it. I haven’t really noticed that being a problem. Does that answer what you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you worry that eating a normal meal will cause you to gain weight?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s really it. I’m not usually thinking, “If I eat this meal, I’ll gain weight.” It’s more that I just… don’t always feel up to eating much. But the weight gain worry isn’t something I’ve noticed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating is ever influenced by a fear of not being able to stop once you start?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so, no. I mean, I don’t really have that feeling of, “If I start eating I won’t be able to stop.” It’s more… I just eat what I can, I guess. I haven’t noticed that fear being a part of it. Is that the kind of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever worry that if you start eating a normal amount, you won't be able to stop eating?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think that’s something I worry about. I mean, once I’m eating I can usually stop normally… I’m not afraid it’ll get out of control or anything. At least, I haven’t noticed that. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious about eating because you might lose control over how much you eat?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn’t say I feel anxious about that. I don’t really worry that I’ll lose control once I start eating. It’s more straightforward than that, I think… but I haven’t noticed that kind of fear. Does that answer it okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like eating a normal meal could lead to eating too much?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t really think of it that way. A normal meal doesn’t make me feel like it would turn into eating too much. I mean… I suppose I don’t always think about it that carefully, but I haven’t noticed that fear. Is that what you’re asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would be upset if you gained back any weight you've lost?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I’d be especially upset about gaining weight back, no. I don’t know if that answers what you’re asking.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating habits are related to trying to look a certain way?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think it’s really about trying to look a certain way. Is that the sort of thing you mean?\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your eating habits change when you're feeling emotions like boredom, happiness, or anger?\n\nPatient: Maybe a little, but I wouldn’t say it’s like happiness or anger makes me eat more or anything. Does that make sense?\n\nDoctor: Are you afraid of gaining weight back to your original weight before the inconsistent eating started?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’m afraid of going back to my original weight.
